# Proposed OEIS comments: A232952 to A234828

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A232952

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A232952>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [2236](../papers/02001-02500/02236-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 78*a(n-1) -1043*a(n-2) +2012*a(n-3) +10236*a(n-4) -39726*a(n-5) +41724*a(n-6) -6408*a(n-7) -8680*a(n-8) +2592*a(n-9) -96*a(n-10)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 10 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 10. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A233017

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A233017>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10829](../papers/10501-11000/10829-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 33 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 71 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 33, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A233085

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A233085>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3015](../papers/03001-03500/03015-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 49*a(n-1) -450*a(n-2) +1466*a(n-3) -1853*a(n-4) +789*a(n-5) for n>8

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. The array grows sideways, so it has to be counted a column at a time, but the clause about which value turns up first is stated in row major order, and a walk across columns does not read the cells in that order. What the walk can carry is, for each named value, the lowest row it has turned up in so far, together with the order those lowest rows were reached; the clause itself is checked only at the end, since a value's lowest row can still drop later. That makes a(n) a walk count on 199 states, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence and checking the one above is a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A233101

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A233101>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2167](../papers/02001-02500/02167-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 25*a(n-1) +52*a(n-2) -1084*a(n-3) +16*a(n-4) +9754*a(n-5) -16165*a(n-6) +5229*a(n-7) +5498*a(n-8) -3740*a(n-9) +320*a(n-10) +96*a(n-11) for n>12

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. The array grows sideways, so it has to be counted a column at a time, but the clause about which value turns up first is stated in row major order, and a walk across columns does not read the cells in that order. What the walk can carry is, for each named value, the lowest row it has turned up in so far, together with the order those lowest rows were reached; the clause itself is checked only at the end, since a value's lowest row can still drop later. That makes a(n) a walk count on 163 states, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence and checking the one above is a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 12. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A233219

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A233219>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3001](../papers/03001-03500/03001-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 111*a(n-1) -2128*a(n-2) +10532*a(n-3) -17559*a(n-4) +9045*a(n-5) for n>7

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. The array grows sideways, so it has to be counted a column at a time, but the clause about which value turns up first is stated in row major order, and a walk across columns does not read the cells in that order. What the walk can carry is, for each named value, the lowest row it has turned up in so far, together with the order those lowest rows were reached; the clause itself is checked only at the end, since a value's lowest row can still drop later. That makes a(n) a walk count on 324 states, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence and checking the one above is a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical recurrence of order 70 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 243 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A234269

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A234269>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [10952](../papers/10501-11000/10952-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence: -(n+1)*(2*n-5)*a(n) -2*n*(n-4)*a(n-1) +4*(n-1)*(2*n-3)*a(n-2) +2*(8*n^2-32*n+29)*a(n-3) +4*(n-2)*(2*n-7)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jan 24 2020

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 3, so the recurrence holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A234545

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A234545>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [343](../papers/00001-00500/00343-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 49 (see link above).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1296 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 49, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

```
